ParlaMint-AT_2002-03-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00098_d7e4566	Please answer me, Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-03-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00098_d7e4582	Ladies and gentlemen! The reform of financial management clearly pursues several objectives: We want to get closer to citizens, we want to be more efficient, we want to be more cost-effective. But of course we also want to work more employee-oriented than is possible today, and, as a result, we can guarantee a uniformity of taxation. ParlaMint-AT_2002-03-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00098_d7e4600	A division of the reply is not actually foreseen, either the State Secretary or the Federal Minister answers. But let's do it that way! Please, Mr State Secretary.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-03-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00098_d7e4627	Mr President. Honourable Members! We do not – I would like to put it forward at the beginning – close any locations of financial offices. Such closures have been made among our predecessors; we know that this is very bad for the population. We therefore foresee that the 80 financial offices currently held by the Republic will be grouped into so-called economic areas, in 43 economic areas. And in these 43 economic areas, the existing financial offices – each one to three financial offices per economic location – will work together as if they were a financial office. What will the improvement consist of? – We want to speed up processes with the help of the Internet. We want to simplify the laws by deregulation, also simplify the legal texts. We want, above all, to use electronics, almost an electronic financial officer, in order, on the one hand, to deepen individual officials in special cases, where it is really necessary, and on the other hand to provide more targeted advice to citizens, especially the single earners, but also the so-called SMEs, i.e. hairdressers or flower dealers, than before. We are now in the phase in which we are making the first attempts with this new system, that is the so-called piloting phase. We will be holding workshops with our staff representatives next week and will carry out these pilot projects again so that staff representation is also fully involved, as it is important to us that we win the employees for this reform. We have foreseen that in the regions of Urfahr, Freistadt, Rohrbach and Eisenstadt, Oberwart, we will make such a piloting effort for the entire financial sector, as well as another ten to twelve so-called theme pilots, trying out individual areas of a financial office, such as an information center. When these pilot projects are successfully completed, we expect to be able to start the reform from 2003 onwards. According to our current ideas, we can complete the reform by 2005 and then provide that an estimated annual savings of € 250 million can be achieved in full through a streamlined, flattered hierarchy and the creation of priorities. - Thank you.

We flatten the hierarchies, that is, we also reorganise the financial country management and relocate the management of the budget and economics into the new financial offices in the economic zones. This will be budgeted out of the ordinary budget, so it will be covered and an important focus of this federal government.

Climate change would be blamed, says American scientists. They assume that in the future several ice caps could break off this dimension. American satellites have thus found out, and American scientists claim that. This is interesting in so far as American policy under George Bush does not want to live up to its original commitment to reduce greenhouse gases by 7 percent in the United States, and it is here that it has failed. From this perspective, the ratification of the Kyoto Protocol can be seen. The ratification of the Kyoto Protocol, which makes Austria the third country in Europe, writes environmental history: This is the first UN agreement on environmental policy to be binding on international law and to impose sanctions. The Protocol provides for a total reduction in the emissions of six greenhouse gases from industrialized countries by at least 5 percent in the period 2008 to 2012 compared to 1990 to 1995. The industrialised countries contribute to this overall reduction according to a differentiated commitment key. The EU as a whole has to reduce at least 8 per cent, with a 13 per cent share in Austria. This represents a high reduction target, which is because Austria already has very low emission rates in international comparison, an ambitious objective in which the implementation of domestic measures is an absolute priority. In order to achieve this objective, comprehensive education of opinion and awareness is necessary in order to help those opportunities already available for breakthrough. I should like to refer to some of the measures that have been implemented or adopted so far. Firstly, the Waste Management Act with the waste economic principle "Pre-disposal prevention". Secondly, Promoting climate protection within the framework of the Federal Environment Promotion. Thirdly, Biomass funding is 483 million Schilling in 2002. Fourth: Improving the heat-related standards of countries for buildings. Fifth, you open a bottle Support programmes of countries for energy-saving measures in new housing and renovation, e.g. energy ID, energy indicators, promotion of ecological building materials. Sixteenths: The priority of the countries for environmental protection, in particular in favour of public transport. Seventeenths: The Electricity Economic and Organisation Act with the target value of 4 percent eco electricity by the year 2007. Eighth: Implementation of the EU Directive on the labelling of new cars with regard to CO2 emissions and so on. Despite these comprehensive measures, it has to be noted that the emissions of the six relevant greenhouse gases in Austria increased by 2.6 percent. This is why an expert paper has been prepared and, footing it, a clear climate strategy has been developed. It is a mix of regulatory measures, promotion measures and voluntary agreements. It must be clearly and clearly stated that the main energy savings potentials are in the area of space heating and transport. If we want the world to remain in balance, we need to shift its weight towards measures that are to be described as regulatory measures: improved building regulations from an ecological and energetic point of view, thus promoting according to the energy characteristics, changing heating systems from fossil energy sources to renewable, increased use of solar energy and wind energy, further adaptations within the framework of the ElWOG, implementation of the landfill regulations and so on. The second point is the promotion measures. Here there has to be a rework in favour of ecological construction and above all renovation in the old house area, which benefits our commercials. [[Congratulation with the ÖVP and the Abg. Ing. Fallent.]] In the case of housing development, a massive increase in the environmental promotion of the federal government and biomass promotion is essential. Thirdly, Fiscal measures in the course of the tax reform, optimization in view of the Kyoto target, for example reduced tax rates for insulating substances and renewable energy sources. According to a Wifo study, all these Kyoto-related measures add 25 000 jobs for Austria – jobs that benefit rural areas in particular. Dear ladies and gentlemen! Together, this ambitious objective, which we set today in the course of ratification, is achievable. And I am convinced that under the leadership of our Federal Minister, Willi Molterer, we will do the same!

ParlaMint-AT_2002-03-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00098_d7e13006	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! I believe, therefore, that this Kyoto Protocol can also serve as a model for future environmental solutions that we have to seek at global level. The European Union has always acted and acted in the lead in the Kyoto issue, and I would say that it is due to the European Union and to our tenacity that we are now finally dealing with a legally binding protocol under the Rio Convention. There are also some areas where Austria has been leading, and I believe that we can be proud that we have achieved, for example, that nuclear energy is not counted on the flexible instruments in respect of the achievement of the goal. Kyoto means being fully active if we want to achieve the goal. Secondly, I still note that there is often a misunderstanding that the implementation of Kyoto would have a negative impact on the economic location in the long term. The opposite is the case: At the same time, that country – I am firmly convinced – which is successful in climate protection is investing in the economic location in the long term. Whoever doubts this, I would like to plausify it with a small practical example. Last week we sat together with the Bavarian Minister for Agriculture, who explained that 70 percent of the funding given by Bavaria for renewable energy flows to Austria. Why? – Because it’s Austrian boiler manufacturers that offer the highest technology – just think about pellets technology! – and there is therefore a real export hit that is stimulated by these innovative companies. Pirklhuber.]] Where are we now? – We are currently on the German side at the official level with the climate strategy based on the work of the Kyoto Forum. Of course, it is right that some of these are linked to additional resources. I told you that, from my point of view, the 2003 budget negotiations will include a key issue, and this is the necessary funding of climate action, because it is also necessary to say clearly: What we do not comply with climate change during the first commitment period we would have to do in the second commitment period. It is therefore purely economic and ecologically necessary to implement the objectives in the first period. At the country level, the question of housing promotion is beyond dispute. The final level and – what I value – the quality of the use of funds are determined, because there is no contribution to climate protection by means of discoloration actions. The third key question, ladies and gentlemen, is the development of the funding instrument. A step has been taken with the novelle of the Environmental Promotion Act, which from my point of view was correct and necessary and with which we also focus quite specifically on climate protection. We will implement fiscal measures – for example, road pricing for trucks, which is out of dispute – as part of the overall road map – Mr Schweitzer has pointed out, a ratio between rail and road of 2: 1, a ratio that we need, we will implement the EU directive on the use of biofuels, and I believe that the CO2 emission labelling directive is also a very important measure. We have made the necessary policy position in the Waste Management Act, just as in biomass production and in many other areas, such as renewable energy. A word on the subject of ElWOG. I believe that the ElWOG must be further developed if we are to implement the EU Directive, which requires that we have a share of 5.75 percent of biofuels in fuels. For this, the ElWOG is not enough with the 4 percent. We need a higher target commitment, and I also appeal to the countries that we focus more on the transnational pooling of renewable electricity in this context. In conclusion: If we want to implement Kyoto successfully – and we still have a lot of way ahead of us – then we must confess that there is no single area of policy where there must not be a contribution to the achievement of Kyoto. No one can take advantage of it!

ParlaMint-AT_2002-03-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00098_d7e13635	The next speaker is Mr Dobnigg. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-03-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00098_d7e13657	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! As we know, the use of fossil energy sources has increased the concentration of greenhouse gas CO2 in the atmosphere by about 30 percent since the beginning of the industrial age. In direct relation, the numerous research results available by renowned scientists speak of a causal link between this increased concentration and a significant warming of the Earth's atmosphere. It is projected to warm up to 5.8 degrees to the end of this century. The likely consequences of this change are an increase in sea level, changes in rainfall and distribution, the melting of glaciers, the migration of species of animals and plants, an intensification of the already existing lack of fresh water in the hot regions of the earth, a accumulation of weather disasters and thus a accumulation of human tragedies, for example in the case of floods of land, as well as movements of migration caused by climate change, and thus the risk of accumulation of armed conflicts around settlement areas. The introduction of this scenario would ultimately lead to unforeseen consequences for all mankind, whether in the field of the environment, the supply of vital goods such as fresh water and food or economic policy. All sectors of life would be affected by the negative consequences of such developments. Because of the global nature of this problem, a coordinated international approach is essential. Austria must therefore also contribute to the reduction of greenhouse gases and has committed itself in the Community with the other countries of the European Union to a minus 8% reduction target in the period 2008 to 2012 compared to the 1990 values. Taking into account the different economic developments in the EU regions, Austria has agreed to a reduction of up to 13 percent compared to 1990. This is an objective that will be very difficult to reach for Austria from today's point of view. For example, in 1999, CO2 emissions were 7 percent higher than those of 1990, and they are still on the rise. In other words, greenhouse gas emissions need to be reduced by almost 19 percent in relation to today's level. Nevertheless, to date – Mr Ulli Sima has already pointed out – a decision on a national climate strategy is missing. Unfortunately, instead of concrete measures, there are only vague statements of intent from this Federal Government. In particular, it shows the need for the immediate introduction of the truck toll to relocate the climate-damaging heavy traffic as much as possible from the road to the environmentally friendly rail. This is an hour's commandment, especially in terms of the enlargement of the European Union to the east and the associated increase in truck traffic, considering that road traffic in Austria alone has increased by around 35 percent in the last ten years and that all forecasts predict further strong increases. The rail is far more efficient than all road transport. It is thus the way out of the line between the ecologically necessary reduction of emissions and the economically necessary maintenance and improvement of transport performance. In other words, it must be invested more and more in the ÖBB. Instead of domestic climate protection measures, this Austrian Federal Government now clearly relies on the so-called flexible mechanisms, i.e. on the acquisition of emission rights abroad. After having fallen asleep in this area until today, the government wants to play the climate-krösus abroad with money that could be better invested in Austria. This gives the Austrian Federal Government not least the opportunity to create urgently needed domestic jobs and boost our domestic economy. According to Wifo's study, up to 25,000 new jobs could be created in your statements, and you, Mr. Federal Minister, have confirmed this in your statements. Despite currently the highest unemployment rates, this opportunity has so far been disgracefully rejected. However, it will also be necessary to finally ensure the financing of Austrian climate protection in cooperation and not, as at present, in confrontation with the federal states. We from the Socialist Group clearly support the ratification of this Kyoto Protocol to safeguard our habitat earth. We bear responsibility for our offspring, for our children, grandchildren and great - grandsons. They too have a right to live in a beautiful country and in a healthy environment.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-03-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00098_d7e14022	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister for Foreign Affairs! Ladies and gentlemen! Austrian criminal law is bound by the principle of universality. This means that the Republic of Austria has a criminal claim against anyone who owns Austrian citizenship and commits a criminal act. It is perfectly unique whether the suspicious action has been committed in Austria or abroad. If, for example, an Austrian citizen in Alaska commits a crime, there is suspicion of a crime, the Republic of Austria, although the crime scene was not in Austria, will in this case in the United States of America, raise a criminal claim, seek extradition and more. [[Abg. Parnigoni: Even though it was in Canada!]] This universality claim of the Republic of Austria naturally conflicts with the claim for territoriality, namely the principle of crime, which we also respect. If a foreign citizen commits a criminal act in Austria, he is also persecuted in Austria. This is similar in most other civilised countries. If an Austrian citizen commits a criminal act abroad, he is also persecuted there and is not likely to be handed over to the Republic of Austria. I can generally point out that, if there is a suspicion that they have committed crimes abroad, Austrian citizens are involved in criminal proceedings – if they can be innocent too – and even if they have finally been sentenced by a foreign criminal court, as Austrian citizens are still under a certain care of the Republic. So this also applies if they have been sentenced. For example, our Austrian ambassador to Cuba, when I had a private stay there about a year ago, reported to me in Havana that it is one of her most important tasks to work for Austrian offenders, for example with regard to prison conditions et cetera. With the conditions of imprisonment abroad, it is truly not the best in many cases, and they are often suitable to actually shorten the lives of an Austrian citizen who was sentenced abroad and who is punishing his punishment there. I think it is a very important task of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, the Austrian ambassador, the representations, to go wherever Austrians are involved in criminal acts or are even suspected of accepting these persons. That is what I would like to say in the opening of the general duty to take care of the general care of the state towards Austrian citizens who are suspected of having committed criminal acts abroad. If, in the course of peacekeeping and maintaining operations abroad, Austrian citizens are suspected of having committed criminal acts, it must be differentiated. In the case of soldiers, there is a corresponding agreement, a statute which puts these soldiers in full immunity. In other words, Whether they commit the suspicious criminal act there in the exercise of their profession or during their spare time, they are completely under immunity, and the Republic of Austria may, on such a stay abroad, demand repatriation to leave Austria – and not extradition because foreign power does not even have the opportunity to arrest in this case. It is a very important and useful privilege that soldiers enjoy. I am convinced that without this privilege there would have been no peacekeeping operations to this extent in the last 20, 30 years. [[Abg. Parnigoni: Where is the urgency?]] Differentiating is the case that in the context of peacekeeping operations, not only soldiers are used. The idea that only soldiers are used is in line with old thinking. The protection of this group of persons is quite different compared to that of the soldiers. They enjoy a much lower level of protection and a limited immunity, that is, only professional immunity. Only if they commit a criminal offence in the course of their professional activity will they be subject to immunity, and of course it is always a question of demarcation: What is another professional task, and what is no longer a job? ParlaMint-AT_2002-03-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00098_d7e14205	In order to give an opinion on the subject-matter of the urgent request, the Federal Minister for Foreign Affairs is speaking. Your speaking time should not exceed 20 minutes. - Please, Mrs. Federal Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-03-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00098_d7e14226	Mr President. High house! Today, the government parties have made a urgent request to improve the legal status of members of the executives and civilians in the framework of UN missions. I had already instructed the Austrian representation to the United Nations to point out to the UN the need to examine the different legal status – which is under debate today – of civilian and military personnel in such operations. This is particularly about strengthening the role of the sending States in the event of alleged infringements by members of their quotas. I therefore particularly welcome this urgent request because Parliamentary support of this project naturally gives added weight. The case of this Austrian UNMIK politician Martin A. in Kosovo, who, as you know and as has just been said, was repatriated due to danger in the delay of Austria – by me, after a request from the Minister of the Interior – has unfortunately shown very clearly that civil protection, especially in the case of police working abroad in the context of international UN operations, is clearly a bad situation compared to the military. As stated in today's urgent request, for example, for military persons in the so-called State of Forces Agreement, it is generally established that they continue to be subject exclusively to the criminal jurisdiction of the issuing State. However, police in international use have to be subject to comparable rules of local criminal jurisdiction, and unfortunately this criminal jurisdiction is not always guaranteed to comply with all human and procedural standards. This is a real inequality between military and civilian personnel, and this is certainly not justified in view of the often overarching tasks and functions of peacekeeping operations. In this context, I would like to make it clear that it is not a question of withdrawing suspects from prosecution, since any criminal offences are, of course, to be suspected of by the authorities of the sending States. Rather, it is about that for those Austrians, who as civilians on a voluntary basis – and that is what it is about: on a voluntary basis! – participate in these peacekeeping operations of the United Nations, an equal legal protection, i.e. a criminal law procedure in accordance with all European human rights standards is explicitly guaranteed by Austria. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] With regard to the case of this UNMIK politician, one must be realistic. It will hardly be possible to achieve a formal change in the current legal status, privileges and immunities of KFOR and UNMIK members in Kosovo in the short term, since, as you know, these regulations are based on Security Council resolution 1244/99. However, in this case, the illegal arrest of UNMIK police officer Martin A. did not even comply with the existing immunity rules by the local UNMIK authorities. I've already done this. For this reason, I have instructed the Austrian delegation in New York to point out to the United Nations, with emphasis on this illegal procedure of the local UNMIK authorities in relation to the immunity of Austrian officials enjoying immunity. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Austria has now asked the UNMIK authorities, but above all the international prosecutor in Kosovo, to cooperate with the Austrian authorities in the investigations in this case. With regard to the process of UNMIK authorities vis-à-vis the Austrian UNMIK politician, Austria has called for the immediate initiation of internal investigations and, above all, legal and disciplinary steps, especially against the responsible UNMIK leaders, but also for the establishment of an independent committee of inquiry with Austrian participation. Finally, Austria has proposed an evaluation of the different legal status of military and civilian persons in peacekeeping operations of the United Nations. In this context, we will be particularly committed to strengthening the role of the issuing State in the event of the absence of immunity. This means that, unlike in the case of Martin A, the United Nations Secretary-General may refrain from immunity only after prior consent or at least consultation of the issuing State. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Ladies and gentlemen! These requirements are clearly in full compliance with those of the 8th century. March, i.e. recently adopted report by the UN Special Committee on Peacekeeping Operations. In this it is said – I now give a translation of the English text – that: The Special Committee reiterates once again that the Member States concerned are fully involved and that they should be provided with timely copies of all internal surveys and investigations of the United Nations, including the final results achieved, with regard to their personnel of incidents where the identification of a criminal debt is likely. - Quote. In the case of future peace missions, I will therefore be particularly concerned with working together with our partners in the European Union to ensure that the legal status of civilians and police from the EU countries is regulated in a joint agreement between the EU and the United Nations within the framework of international UN operations. In the context of future international operations, such an agreement will be particularly important in the context of the current civil EU-Politimeruppe – which is at least large troops – because such a regulation and adequate legal protection are essential – and this was also expressed in the letter written by Dr. Pfanzelter – that, in the future, Austrians will continue to volunteer for such operations. This is the basis for the often recognized and praised Austrian efforts in peace operations for which the UN Secretary-General was not long ago awarded the Nobel Peace Prize, in which, of course, all of us are included. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] I say: The security of Austrian volunteers is an essential concern for me, not only in the specific case. Sometimes it's not a non-hazardous use. The UN also knows what it has in Austria, and has always stressed this. For example, the United Nations Under-Secretary-General for Peacekeeping Operations, Jean-Marie Guéhenno, took part in a solemn act on 14 May. March 2002, i.e. a week ago, the Austrian UN Ambassador Dr. Pfanzelter presented the Dag-Hammarskjöld Medal. This medal is awarded to those who unfortunately had to live their lives in the course of their mission for international peace and security. Ambassador Dr. Pfanzelter has taken up the medal in support of 21 families of Austrian UN soldiers who have lost their lives in the performance of their service, and, I would like to expressly say this, has thanked Guéhenno Austria, on behalf of the United Nations, for the use of over 50,000 soldiers and police who have already participated in peacekeeping operations. A UN representative who addressed Austrian press releases in this context, who said that the UN would not appreciate Austrian contributions to peacekeeping, said clearly in an official statement, and it was really a high-ranking UN representative that he once again referred to the comments of the UN Secretary-General to Federal Minister Scheibner, who had been in New York, on which occasion the Secretary-General of Austria assured the highest possible appreciation for his long-term commitment to peacekeeping and expressed the hope that Austria would continue to do so in the future. ParlaMint-AT_2002-03-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00098_d7e15599	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Any Austrian citizen can expect the Austrian Federal Government to exercise its interests if it operates in another country within the framework of a United Nations humanitarian and peacekeeping commitment. The case of Austrian police officer Martin A., who worked in Kosovo as part of the UN mission, has clearly shown that our government – and above all our foreign minister – also takes on potential difficulties to protect the lives of an Austrian citizen. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] I express my thanks to Mrs Ferrero-Waldner for this gesture of humanity, for her sense of responsibility and duty. Today, however, it is also a matter of learning something from this case, namely that it must never happen again that Austrians who want to make peace in a crisis area in another country are in conflict with local criminal jurisdiction, which does not correspond to that of the country of origin, or even by means of government arbitrariness, must expect freedom-restriction measures. The reason for today's urgent request is that exclusively soldiers working under the UN enjoy immunity under the Staff Regulations Agreement. Civil society, such as executive officials, doctors or teachers, who increasingly opt for UN peacekeeping and humanitarian use, enjoy only functional immunity. As we have seen, this can lead to dramatic human rights violations. This problem has to be solved without delay. In the case of UN missions in the field of education or executive officials, as we have seen, only reduced protection has been given. I would therefore like to thank the Foreign Minister for responding immediately and calling for the legal equality of civilians in the UN mission with the UN soldiers. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] What is regulated by the UN military statute agreement for soldiers must also apply to civilians, namely, all of them subject to the criminal jurisdiction of the home country. As long as respect for human rights in crisis areas is not ensured, this absolute equality must be between soldiers and civilians and the UN executive standard. Especially in Kosovo, but also in other areas of crisis in which Austrians are peacefully active within the UN, there is no comparable legal certainty. As human rights spokesperson of my party, I would point out that there is also no respect for the European Convention on Human Rights in these crisis areas. We are dealing here with countries that are still far from meeting European human rights standards or complying with human rights. Unfortunately, this is the situation and, among other things, UN operations are also used to gradually implement the human rights convention in these regions. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! Imagine, a friend of yours has opted for such a commitment – whether as a teacher, doctor or police officer – and, on account of any incident caused by local government arbitrariness, is held in the local prison without any clear charges, without more detailed investigation, possibly together with criminals he has carried out several days ago. Faith Do you really think that only one Austrian will be willing to participate in such UN operations in the future? - I don't think so. It is therefore so important that all Austrian citizens, no matter what profession or status they have at home, are legally treated and enjoy the protection of the Austrian rule of law. It is necessary to extend immunity protection to civilians in the UN deployment. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Over 50,000 Austrians have been active in the service of the UN over the last 40 years. Austria is a respected partner of the UN. It is therefore also our right to bring in concerns concerning the protection of our countrymen. I am sure that Mrs Foreign Minister Ferrero-Waldner will succeed with Austria's concern for a transitional solution with the Secretary-General of the United Nations. In this transitional solution, all executive officials and civilians present in the UN must receive absolute immunity protection – the same as UN soldiers. However, it is also important to find common solutions with the United Nations within the framework of the European Union. The legal improvement of the situation for members of the executives and civilians must also be of concern to the other European states; I am deeply convinced of that. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Our foreign minister has dealt with her countrymen in the sense of humanity and loyalty. However, it has also made a forward-looking decision. With the call for an approximation of the immunity status to that of soldiers in the UN mission, Austria is a model for improvements in the sense of human rights, and Mrs Foreign Minister Ferrero-Waldner is our representative, who is fully committed to the protection of Austrian citizens. I especially thank her for trying to continue to behave like this, and since this is the beginning of spring, I can hand over a small flowerstock to her. – Thank you very much!

ParlaMint-AT_2002-03-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00098_d7e17172	Mr President. Minister! High house! As early as March 1985, exactly 17 years ago, the first climate conference was held in Villach, the culmination of which was the signing of the Vienna Convention for the Protection of the Ozone Layer and which was also prepared for the United Nations Climate Change Convention. In 1992 the contract was signed in Rio de Janeiro. At that time, the important foundations for international climate change were laid, which, by means of the Kyoto Protocol, for the first time received an internationally binding framework, which is now in place to ratify this plenary session. It has been ten years since Rio de Janeiro that an international agreement has been reached that the industrialised countries should have stabilised their greenhouse gas emissions at 1990 level by the year 2000. When, in the mid-1990s, it became known and reckable that virtually all industrialized countries, with the exception of the reform states affected by the economic collapse, would miss this goal, it was especially the United States under President Clinton and Vice-President Gore, who in Kyoto insisted on adopting a legally binding international agreement on the reduction of greenhouse gases. Economic analyses have shown that the implementation of the Protocol will cost the United States the most. However, these analyses also show that non-implementation of the Protocol at the economic level would take place, above all, the European Union. What it would mean in Austria, for example, ladies and gentlemen, if, for example, winter tourism were to suffer damage due to a lack of snow, I do not need to mention it here. On the Austrian side, the then Minister for the Environment Bartenstein took part in Japan in 1997, and he had set very high targets. Austria should promise a 20 to 25 percent reduction in greenhouse gas emissions in Kyoto, but the industrial union inspectors would then ensure that Austria will have to reduce only 8 percent in accordance with Kyoto Protocol and 13 percent in accordance with EU internal agreement. Ladies and gentlemen! Until two years ago, the Federal Government of this country was actually in the process of drawing up action plans with the countries and, above all, financing these action plans. Where are we today? – Instead of at least 1.25 billion schilling annually as an incentive promotion, only a fraction of this sum is available. [[Federal Minister Mag. Molterer: More than ever before!]] Minister! In the space heating area, where the greatest savings potential would exist, about one third of the necessary reductions could be achieved. A proper old house renovation programme, for example, could also provide important impulses for the medium-sized economy that is really not spoiled by the zero deficit. But the funds of the federal government are not increased, but only re-storyed, and the countries are expected to raise the necessary funds. The second major sector, which decides on success or failure in implementing the Protocol, is transport. According to Kyoto option analysis, this sector is expected to contribute almost a quarter of the necessary savings. At the same time, however, transport experts predict that passenger and freight transport will not decrease by 2010 but will increase significantly. There is an increase of 35 percent here. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, only with a massive shift in heavy road transport from road to rail and with the increased expansion of public transport between cities and cities can the Kyoto Protocol be established. Minister! Nevertheless, according to official data, more than 14,000 whales have been killed since then. The whalemen from two highly industrialized member countries of the IWC continue to kill whales for human consumption: In both Norway and Japan, whale meat is sold as expensive delicacy. However, it is also important that Japan kill whales even in the Antarctic protection area in the context of a derogation under the pretext of science and then declare this a scientific method. Ladies and gentlemen! Whales are the largest mammals on earth. People are responsible for the threat of whale extinction. For centuries the whaling was a huge business, and only in the mid-1970s opposed the thoughtless plundering of the seas. We must therefore be concerned about the protection of biodiversity, the maintenance of the ecological process and the living systems. The biodiversity offers an invaluable genetic reservoir, which is essential for medical, biological, agricultural and scientific progress. Especially in this area is the need to live always in concern for nature, because the extinction of a single species can lead to a gap in the overall structure of nature, whose consequences for the ecological balance are unmistakable. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen! Nature is threatened, and this is evident in a dramatic way in the development of biodiversity. For this reason, the countries need to join forces in the 20th to 24th years. ==References====External links== The annual meeting of the International Whaling Commission is to be held in Veto. We will give this request our consent.

I would first of all like to address the process of enlargement, which is so important to our country in particular. In 1993 the Copenhagen European Council established binding criteria for the candidate countries, according to which democracy, the rule of law, respect for human rights and respect for and protection of minorities must be achieved in order to be able to join the European Union at all. Compliance with these standards, but also common commitment to partnership cooperation in these matters is essential. However, I also believe that all states wishing to be members of the European community of values must assume moral responsibility for historical injustice. Just as Austria takes on its historical responsibility, so must our northern and eastern neighbours. Another aspect which is clearly reflected in this motion for a resolution is the active international cooperation between governments and institutions on the global enforcement of human rights. In this context, Federal Minister Ferrero-Waldner also introduced two draft resolutions from Austria concerning the protection of minorities and the protection of refugees. Austrian human rights policy is committed both nationally and internationally to human rights education, the effective fight against racism and xenophobia, the protection of minorities, the abolition of the death penalty, the elimination of trafficking in human beings and the situation of internally displaced persons. Austria is primarily committed to international cooperation and dialogue. National singles, even though they would often be desirable and would meet the personal commitment of human rights activists, often bring very little in this sensitive area and can sometimes destroy more than build up. Of course, international human rights policy is being made at different levels, in different forums. Of course, I cannot go into all levels, but I would like to take some essential points out. I would like to stress here that these processes are constantly also requiring rapid reactions and opinions to help take the ideas presented by Austria into account. However useful it is that different forums also deal with human rights policy, the example of the death penalty shows: Of course, the Union can act quite differently from what is possible, for example, only within the framework of the United Nations. The Union has, as you know, repeatedly demarcated in a large number of cases against the United States in terms of death sentences, especially when it has been dealing with threatened executions of young people or mentally ill persons, and has partly joined the various court proceedings as "amicus curiae". In recent months, general demarches have also been intensified for the abolition of the death penalty or at least for a moratorium on the execution of the death penalty, especially against African and Asian countries, including countries such as China, Saudi Arabia, Japan, Thailand and Nigeria. – We have also intervened against torture and, above all, for the rights of women and children. Let me take a particular case in this regard, which is a very touching one, and which, I believe, all of us are of course extremely disturbed: the case of Mrs Safya Hussini. You know this is the Nigerian woman who should suffer the death penalty due to stoning due to an unmarried pregnancy in the last consequence. – I can only tell you my personal concern: My first reaction was of course to inform me personally of any development in this case immediately, then to contact the lawyers of this woman and to get their assessment of the case, and then, above all, to actively, here again, of course, in the EU framework, to examine all those steps with which this woman can be helped. But now, of course, the problem of this case – and I would like to address the complexity of human rights on the basis of this case – is very difficult. In fact, this woman could be more at risk of swift action, so we have to make a careful assessment of the case. Two national factors exist in Nigeria itself: On the one hand, this case is taking place in the north of Nigeria, in the state of Sokoto. There is a strained relationship between central government and federal authorities. The central government is against the execution of the death penalty, it is also aware of the international attitude and has provided three lawyers for the defence of Mrs. Hussini. On the other hand, in the Islamic North provinces, as you know, the Sharia is introduced, and one wants to sit there an example. – There is a quadruple of instance. ==References====External links== March, i.e. on this Monday, but that was only the court hearing of the second instance, that is, it is still an instance to take. He literally says: "There are procedures in the first instance." In these words, Ambassador Gruša, a well-known representative of the Czech Republic, admits to an Austrian daily that the Beneš decrees are not dead wrong, as is always claimed. This shows to be true the position that the Freedom Party has always taken on this issue. It is therefore necessary to take this opportunity to adapt the position taken by the Federal Government in this regard. - That's just for information. It is in this interview with a "knowledge" in a way that is unworthy of an ambassador. Ambassador Gruša obviously has no understanding of international law and has no basic knowledge in international law, if he answers the question of whether it is property rights or the expropriation of private property – I quote the essential key point: "If you are fighting a war and are unconditionally capitulating, your" – that is, the Sudeten Germans are meant to be – "claims for it." – That means they're more or less loose. Mrs. Federal Minister! There is an infinite amount of international literature. For example, there is the Hague Land War Code, in which Article 46 lays down the inviolability of private property – that is mandatory by international law – and whose article 47 explicitly states that private property must not be recovered in the event of war and in the case of capitulation. In addition, the Hague Land War Code states that private property must not be plundered or destroyed and – that is crucial! – that civilians from the occupied area should not be dragged away or relocated. – This is applicable international law! Ladies and gentlemen! I do not know how the Republic of Austria would deal with an ambassador who is not aware of the public abroad, I do not know how you, Mrs Federal Minister, would proceed there. I think it is simply naïve when a well-known representative of a state, in this case an ambassador, acts as such. How can one give such nonsensity in a country like Austria? It may be that he did it in ignorance – but that can be dangerous as well. Essentially, it is important, I think, to pay attention to this interview accordingly. It was necessary, and therefore one must be grateful for it. I hope that you, Mrs Bundesminister, will have a discussion with Ambassador Gruša on this point and that you will also draw attention to international standards. Perhaps you can move him to rethink his point of view in this way. It seems to be true that the representatives of the Czech Republic – in this case an ambassador – in the domestic and foreign countries have a way of thinking regarding the humane Beneš decrees or deportation decrees, which, as we have all noted in the meantime in consensus, are in violation of human rights, which is no longer contemporary. To change this, we must work, and I ask you, Mrs Bundesminister, to continue to work in this direction. Thank you.

This is a fact, and it is difficult to describe it as “ethnic cleansing on the basis of collective debt”. One cannot ignore the circumstances under which this happened. At the end of the 1930s, the German minority contributed to the destruction of the ČSR, and the Nazis suffered a great deal of suffering from the inhabitants of the then Protectorate Bohemia and Moravia. The hatred of the Germans and the pursuit of retaliation for previous injustice can be understood psychologically. Understanding, ladies and gentlemen, does not mean justifying.
